Let me create both files."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":877,"cache_read_input_tokens":67798,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":877,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"}},"requestId":"req_011CZ9ge5WmJvT2HBRNztyTn","type":"assistant","uuid":"a709aeb7-f898-4681-be32-3e429ae3b5e1","timestamp":"2026-03-18T01:33:16.786Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"a5125120-9cbe-408a-953a-457fed0eba08","version":"2.1.78","gitBranch":"main","slug":"fluffy-cuddling-tome"}
{"parentUuid":"a709aeb7-f898-4681-be32-3e429ae3b5e1","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a8bfbbbb3719cc3f4","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01276seDrNP5R9nyj7HLougx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01VaFzGK4WXNUdKJBn9Deb2g","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager/mardov-vault/production/music-direction.md","content":"---\ntype: production\ntitle: \"Mardov Campaign — Music Direction Bible\"\ncreated: 2026-03-17\ntags:\n  - music\n  - production\n  - audio-direction\n---\n\n# Mardov Campaign -- Music Direction Bible\n\n## Philosophy\n\nThe Mardov score operates on two simultaneous layers:\n\n1. **Dark Fantasy Orchestral Throughline** -- Non-diegetic. A modern cinematic orchestral score in the vein of Bear McCreary (God of War), Ramin Djawadi (Game of Thrones), and Yoko Shimomura (Kingdom Hearts). This is the backbone. It carries the narrative weight, underscores battles, and provides emotional architecture for all five acts.\n\n2. **Character-Specific Genre Themes** -- Each PC and key NPC has a genre assignment that reflects their personality, arc, and soul. These themes weave into the orchestral throughline: sometimes surfacing as solo moments, sometimes blending beneath the full orchestra, sometimes clashing against it when a character is in conflict with their world.\n\n### The Raven Queen Throughline\n\nA **flute/piccolo motif** serves as the unifying thread across the entire score. This motif represents the Raven Queen, Sabine Ra, fate, and death -- the cosmic forces that bind every character together. It appears:\n\n- As a **distant solo flute** in quiet moments (campfire scenes, travel montages)\n- As a **piccolo trill woven into combat brass** during battle\n- As a **full woodwind choir** during moments of prophecy, death, or cosmic revelation\n- **Inverted/distorted** when Vecna or V-Mardov forces are present\n- In **Sprocket's theme specifically**, since he carries the Raven Queen's Cloak\n\nThe motif should be simple enough to hum: a descending minor phrase, five notes, with an unexpected resolution on the fourth. Think the Shire theme from Lord of the Rings in its versatility, but haunting rather than warm.\n\n### Diegetic vs Non-Diegetic\n\n| Type | Source | Style | Characters |\n|------|--------|-------|------------|\n| **Diegetic** | Bards performing in-world | Period-appropriate acoustic instruments | Randal (singer-songwriter guitar), Frozen Song (lute), tavern musicians |\n| **Non-diegetic** | Score/soundtrack | Modern cinematic orchestral with genre infusions | All other character themes |\n\nWhen a diegetic performer plays, the non-diegetic score should pull back -- letting the in-world music breathe. Transitions between diegetic and non-diegetic should be seamless: a tavern lute melody might gradually acquire orchestral strings beneath it as the scene shifts from casual to dramatic.\n\n---\n\n## Character Theme Specifications\n\n### Daina Fireforge -- Heavy Metal\n\n**Genre**: Heavy metal / doom metal\n**Primary Instruments**: Distorted electric guitar (down-tuned), double bass drum, power chords, anvil strikes\n**Tempo Range**: 100-140 BPM (slower doom riffs when brooding, faster thrash when raging)\n**Key/Mode**: D minor (drop D tuning for guitar -- the metal standard)\n**Mood**: Relentless, volcanic, grieving, unstoppable, forged-in-fire\n\n**How It Integrates**: Daina's metal guitars enter beneath the orchestral strings during combat. When she rages, the orchestra drops out and raw distorted guitar takes over -- then the orchestra crashes back in on the downbeat of her strike. The Raven Queen flute appears as a clean melodic line above her distortion during emotional moments (her father's memory, the Fireforge revelation).\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. Daina does not make music. She IS the music -- the sound of a hammer on an anvil, the roar of a forge at full heat.\n\n**Reference Style**: Think the heaviness of Black Sabbath's \"Iron Man\" crossed with the orchestral doom of Bear McCreary's God of War combat themes. The lava/forge aesthetic from her visual design should be audible -- grinding, molten, immense.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: The anvil strike is literal. Her theme should incorporate metalworking percussion (hammer on anvil, ringing steel) as rhythmic elements. Her Reborn transformation gets a key change -- same riff, shifted up a half step, with added harmonic overtones representing the glowing runes.\n\n---\n\n### Sprocket Overspark -- Electronic\n\n**Genre**: Electronic / chiptune / glitch\n**Primary Instruments**: Synthesizers, arpeggiated sequences, 8-bit textures, glitchy processing, theremin\n**Tempo Range**: 130-160 BPM (frenetic, ticking, always accelerating)\n**Key/Mode**: C major shifting to C minor (starts bright, darkens as fate closes in)\n**Mood**: Manic, brilliant, ticking-clock, chaotic-good, cosmically burdened\n\n**How It Integrates**: Sprocket's electronic textures layer beneath the orchestral score as a persistent rhythmic undercurrent -- like machinery humming under a cathedral. When he casts divination magic, the electronics surge forward. The Raven Queen flute motif is sampled, pitch-shifted, and looped inside his theme -- fate rendered as data.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the sound of a mind that processes reality faster than reality moves.\n\n**Reference Style**: Think Disasterpeace (Fez, It Follows) for the chiptune-meets-cosmic-dread quality. The ticking clock element evokes the \"ultimate answer\" -- his foreknowledge of his own death. Also draws from Undertale's Toby Fox for the way chiptune can carry devastating emotional weight.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: His Portent dice should have a sound -- a crystalline chime that precedes a key change, signaling that fate has been altered. His crystalline wings get ascending arpeggio flourishes. The maid-outfit-building montage gets a jaunty 8-bit interlude.\n\n---\n\n### Brigit Wildfire -- Celtic Music\n\n**Genre**: Celtic / Irish folk\n**Primary Instruments**: Uilleann pipes, bodhr&aacute;n, tin whistle, fiddle, Celtic harp\n**Tempo Range**: 80-130 BPM (slow airs for emotional moments, reels for action)\n**Key/Mode**: D Dorian (the quintessential Celtic mode -- minor feel with a raised 6th)\n**Mood**: Rooted, fierce, nurturing, transformative, ancient\n\n**How It Integrates**: Brigit's Celtic instruments are the most naturally compatible with the dark fantasy orchestral throughline. Her uilleann pipes can double the orchestral oboe lines. Her fiddle weaves into the string section. This makes her theme the emotional glue of the score -- always present, always grounding. When she transforms into her Progenitor form, the Celtic instruments distort: the fiddle becomes dissonant, the pipes become a wailing drone, the bodhr&aacute;n becomes thunderous.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. Though her fire-tending and campfire scenes create natural acoustic spaces where the Celtic instruments feel almost diegetic.\n\n**Reference Style**: Think Bear McCreary's Outlander score for the Celtic orchestral fusion. Keyleth's motifs from Critical Role (Sam Riegel's compositions). The Progenitor transformation should evoke Wardruna -- ancient Nordic/Celtic ritual music that feels pre-human.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: As the player with 107+ session notes, Brigit is the narrator. Her theme should be the most developed, the most varied, the one that appears in the most contexts. The Zodiac Locket gets its own sub-motif within her theme -- a music-box-like Celtic harp figure that plays the Raven Queen motif in a new key. Her Stag form gets low uilleann pipe drones with deep bodhr&aacute;n pulse.\n\n---\n\n### Varis Laindon -- Soft Rock\n\n**Genre**: Soft rock / folk rock / Americana\n**Primary Instruments**: Acoustic guitar (fingerpicked), light electric guitar (clean tone), bass, brushed drums\n**Tempo Range**: 70-110 BPM (steady, patient, watchful)\n**Key/Mode**: G major / E minor (warm but capable of sudden darkness)\n**Mood**: Steady, watchful, melancholic, protective, dawn-light\n\n**How It Integrates**: Varis's soft rock guitar provides a grounding warmth beneath the orchestra. His fingerpicked acoustic patterns create texture during travel and watch scenes. When he draws his bow (the 200+ damage shot), the soft rock builds into a sustained power chord that rings under the full orchestra -- the quiet man's moment of thunder.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the sound of watching a sunrise alone and knowing you might not see the next one.\n\n**Reference Style**: Think early Fleetwood Mac (the Lindsey Buckingham fingerpicking era), blended with Nick Drake's melancholic acoustic work. The \"Knight of the Dusken Wood\" transformation echoes Hozier -- warm voice-like guitar lines that carry grief without breaking.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: His cyan-teal arrows should have a musical signature -- a harmonic overtone on the guitar that shimmers when an arrow is nocked. The Vodram flashback gets the same theme played in a minor key with reverb, like hearing it from the other side of a destroyed world.\n\n---\n\n### Nocturnus Wayne -- Emo\n\n**Genre**: Emo / post-hardcore / gothic rock\n**Primary Instruments**: Distorted guitar (gain cranked), bass (driving eighth notes), crash cymbals, piano (sparse, in breakdowns)\n**Tempo Range**: 90-140 BPM (brooding verses, explosive choruses)\n**Key/Mode**: B-flat minor (dark, dramatic, the key of gothic romance)\n**Mood**: Brooding, explosive, self-destructive, reluctantly noble, razor-edged\n\n**How It Integrates**: Nocturnus's emo guitars clash against the orchestral throughline -- deliberately. His theme is the one that disrupts the score's cohesion, because he disrupts the party's cohesion. When he cooperates (Slow Fall to save Daina, diamond to resurrect Varis), his theme briefly harmonizes with the orchestra before pulling away. The Raven Queen flute plays in counterpoint to his distorted guitar -- fate and rebellion in dialogue.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the sound of what Kingdom Hearts would be if Sora lost.\n\n**Reference Style**: My Chemical Romance's \"The Black Parade\" for the gothic theatrical quality. The Used for the raw, ragged vocal-like guitar tone. For the Empty Body moments (when he becomes translucent and young), switch to Radiohead's \"Exit Music\" -- ethereal, fragile, heartbreaking.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: Bane (his sentient sword) should have its own sub-motif: a low, resonant hum like a cello played with too much pressure, always slightly out of tune. The Nightmare Sword corruption gets atonal, feedback-laden distortion. The pacifying spore covering half his face gets a split-channel effect: emo guitar in one ear, organic ambient drone in the other. \"I FEEL LIKE A GOD\" (absorbing Asmodeus's essence) gets the full My Chemical Romance treatment -- blast beats, screaming guitar, and then sudden silence.\n\n---\n\n### Nyx Khamestria -- Classical Ballet\n\n**Genre**: Classical ballet / romantic-era orchestral\n**Primary Instruments**: Solo violin, celesta, harp, strings (pizzicato for playful moments, legato for emotional)\n**Tempo Range**: 60-120 BPM (waltz time signatures, 3/4 and 6/8)\n**Key/Mode**: F-sharp minor (delicate, melancholic, faintly otherworldly)\n**Mood**: Graceful, melancholic, defiant, fey-touched, heartbreaking\n\n**How It Integrates**: Nyx's ballet theme is the most \"classical\" of the character themes, which means it blends invisibly into the orchestral throughline -- and that invisibility IS her character. She hides in plain sight. Her solo violin surfaces when she is alone or when her true feelings emerge. The celesta represents her fey magic (involuntary, leaking, unsettling). The Raven Queen flute harmonizes naturally with her violin -- two solo instruments trading phrases like sisters who were separated.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. The Knight of the Flower Field persona gets a brighter, major-key variant that sounds almost diegetic -- like music from a children's storybook -- but it is the lie she tells the world.\n\n**Reference Style**: Tchaikovsky's Swan Lake for the duality (white swan/black swan = Nyx/Xyn). Saint-Saens' \"The Swan\" for the solo cello/violin melancholy. Studio Ghibli's Joe Hisaishi for the celesta work (Spirited Away's \"One Summer's Day\"). The fey magic manifesting gets Debussy-like whole-tone scales -- nothing resolves, everything shimmers.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: Her owl Hedwig's death (Ch. 8) should establish a two-note motif (descending minor second) that recurs whenever loss is present. The Xyn connection gets a mirror of Nyx's theme played in inversion -- the same notes, upside down, heard through a portal. Reynardine's presence warps Nyx's theme into a music-box version that slows and distorts.\n\n---\n\n### Stalwart Little Goldentooth -- Country\n\n**Genre**: Country / Americana / southern rock\n**Primary Instruments**: Pedal steel guitar, banjo, acoustic guitar (strummed), fiddle, harmonica\n**Tempo Range**: 90-130 BPM (steady march tempo for conviction, faster for righteous fury)\n**Key/Mode**: A major (bright, confident, uncomplicated -- like his worldview)\n**Mood**: Righteous, stubborn, earnest, protective, sermon-in-steel\n\n**How It Integrates**: Stalwart's country instruments provide an unexpected warmth against the dark fantasy orchestral palette. His pedal steel guitar creates emotional swells beneath battle scenes. The banjo provides rhythmic drive during his \"casting Shield of Faith\" moments. When his divine magic manifests (radiant energy from the shield), the country instruments transition into a gospel choir texture -- southern rock meets divine proclamation.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the sound of a man who has never once doubted that he is right, and the terrifying power that comes from that certainty.\n\n**Reference Style**: Johnny Cash's \"Ring of Fire\" for the unshakable conviction. Chris Stapleton for the raw emotional power. Ennio Morricone's spaghetti western scores for the lone-paladin-on-the-frontier quality. The Shield of Retribution moments get Lynyrd Skynyrd energy -- southern rock that hits like divine judgment.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: Linda (his emotional support chicken) gets a comedic banjo riff that plays whenever she appears. His self-appointed leadership gets a march-tempo snare drum beneath the country instruments. His gold scales catching light should be musically represented by a pedal steel slide -- warm, gleaming, rising.\n\n---\n\n### Bergs Fireforge -- Psychedelic Rock\n\n**Genre**: Psychedelic rock / space rock / prog\n**Primary Instruments**: Fuzz guitar, organ (Hammond B3), wah pedal, bongo drums, sitar (for the myconid/fungal consciousness)\n**Tempo Range**: 70-120 BPM (slow, expansive, hypnotic)\n**Key/Mode**: E Mixolydian (major-ish but with a flat 7th -- earthy, unresolved, cosmic)\n**Mood**: Earthy, expansive, connected, transcendent, dwarf-who-became-a-god\n\n**How It Integrates**: Bergs' psychedelic guitars create a warm, fuzzy haze beneath the orchestral strings -- like looking at a cathedral through heat shimmer. His theme starts grounded and dwarven (heavy, rhythmic, forge-adjacent) but acquires increasingly cosmic textures as his arc progresses. By the Stone Table sacrifice, the psychedelic rock has fully merged with the orchestral throughline -- he IS the score. The Raven Queen flute plays through a phaser effect in his sections.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the sound of mushroom spores carrying consciousness across a continent.\n\n**Reference Style**: Pink Floyd's \"Echoes\" for the cosmic-consciousness quality. Black Sabbath's \"Planet Caravan\" for the gentle, spacey side of heavy music. Beatles' \"Tomorrow Never Knows\" for the sitar/drone elements. Post-ascension, add Tame Impala's modern psychedelic production -- lush, swirling, enveloping.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: The myconid network connection gets a bioluminescent audio texture -- a sustained drone with pulsing, breathing rhythm (like the spores that drift in his wake). Kelemtor (the talking skull) gets a comedic surf-rock riff. The buddy cop duo with Sprocket gets their themes mashed up -- fuzz guitar meets chiptune. The Stone Table ascension should be the score's most psychedelic moment: full Pink Floyd space-rock breakdown dissolving into pure orchestral light.\n\n---\n\n### Randal -- Singer-Songwriter Guitar (DIEGETIC)\n\n**Genre**: Singer-songwriter / acoustic folk\n**Primary Instruments**: Acoustic guitar (fingerpicked and strummed), panflute, voice (implied/hummed), hand drums\n**Tempo Range**: 60-100 BPM (unhurried, contemplative, dying-man's-pace)\n**Key/Mode**: C major / A minor (the most accessible keys -- music for everyone)\n**Mood**: Gentle, wise, urgent-beneath-calm, mortal, watching-the-clock\n\n**How It Integrates**: Randal's theme is DIEGETIC -- he is a bard, and his music exists in the world. When Randal plays, the non-diegetic orchestral score fades to silence, letting his acoustic guitar fill the space. This makes his performances feel sacred -- the world pauses to listen. When his counter-charm wakes Brigit from a mind-flayer stun (Ch. 30), his diegetic guitar seamlessly becomes the non-diegetic score as the scene shifts from intimate to epic.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: **Diegetic.** Randal performs in-world. His lantern staff and traveling minstrel aesthetic are acoustic. Other characters can hear him play.\n\n**Reference Style**: Nick Drake for the fingerpicked melancholy. Iron and Wine for the gentle warmth. The panflute evokes Andean folk music -- high altitude, thin air, spiritual weight. For his \"God of Choice\" divine nature, add a subtle overtone singing quality (Tuvan throat singing) -- one voice containing multitudes.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: His dimensional shackles should produce a faint rhythmic clink that becomes part of his music's percussion. His dying status means his theme should imperceptibly slow across the campaign -- same melody, incrementally fewer BPM. The \"God of Choice\" reveal gets a moment where his simple acoustic guitar suddenly resonates with impossible harmonics, as though a single instrument is producing the sound of many.\n\n---\n\n### Tesca Duskhallow -- Yoga/Meditation Music\n\n**Genre**: Meditation / ambient / world music fusion\n**Primary Instruments**: Singing bowls, tabla, tanpura drone, bamboo flute, finger cymbals, gentle bells\n**Tempo Range**: 50-90 BPM (meditative, breath-paced, flowing)\n**Key/Mode**: D Phrygian (exotic, minor, with a Middle Eastern/South Asian color)\n**Mood**: Centered, flowing, defiant-beneath-stillness, twilight-colored, street-wise-monk\n\n**How It Integrates**: Tesca's meditation textures create pools of calm within the orchestral storm. Her tabla rhythms provide a heartbeat-like pulse beneath action scenes. When she fights (Flurry of Blows, Stunning Strike), the meditation music accelerates into a controlled fury -- the same instruments, same scales, but at combat tempo. The stillness is the threat. The Raven Queen flute harmonizes with her bamboo flute, creating a duet of death (Raven Queen) and discipline (Tesca).\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. Her theme is the sound of a street kid who learned to be still -- and weaponized that stillness.\n\n**Reference Style**: Anoushka Shankar for the sitar/tabla virtuosity. Deuter or Karunesh for the meditative ambient quality. Kung Fu Hustle / Crouching Tiger soundtrack for the way meditation music transforms into martial arts percussion. The monastery training montage gets Shaolin film score energy.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: Her tail (used for balance during kicks) should have a wind-chime sound when it sweeps. The Diadem of Dawn / Anchor of Famine gets a discordant singing bowl -- beautiful but wrong, the sound of a wish that costs everything. Her parents' death flashback gets the meditation music played on broken instruments -- same melody, shattered timbre.\n\n---\n\n### Birt -- Detective Music (Noir Jazz)\n\n**Genre**: Noir jazz / cool jazz / bebop\n**Primary Instruments**: Alto saxophone, muted trumpet, upright bass (walking), brushed snare, piano (cocktail)\n**Tempo Range**: 80-120 BPM (cool, unhurried swagger)\n**Key/Mode**: E-flat major / C minor (classic jazz tonality -- smoky, sophisticated)\n**Mood**: Wry, precise, secretly-brilliant, comedic-but-devastating, birds-eye-view\n\n**How It Integrates**: Birt's jazz creates a tonal contrast with the dark fantasy throughline that is deliberately funny -- a noir detective saxophone playing over a medieval battle is absurd, and Birt IS absurd. But when the comedy drops (the phoenix healing, the high-level devastating magic), the jazz deepens into something serious: the muted trumpet becomes a lament, the walking bass becomes ominous. The Raven Queen flute plays a jazz improvisation -- fate riffing.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the internal monologue of a bird wizard who thinks he is in a different genre entirely.\n\n**Reference Style**: The Cowboy Bebop soundtrack (Yoko Kanno) -- jazz that carries emotional devastation beneath cool swagger. For Birt specifically, add Vince Guaraldi (Peanuts) for the comedic piano moments. Raymond Scott for the \"mad scientist bird\" energy.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: His teleportation (blip in/out with crimson feather scatter) gets a bebop fill -- fast, flashy, then gone. His white dove companion gets a countermelody on clarinet. The phoenix healing scene gets the jazz equivalent of a hymn -- slow, reverent, the saxophone becoming a prayer. Blindness spells get atonal free-jazz bursts.\n\n---\n\n### Josh Coolbreeze -- Heavy Woodwind Orchestral\n\n**Genre**: Heavy woodwind orchestral / wind ensemble\n**Primary Instruments**: Bassoon, contrabassoon, bass clarinet, French horn, tuba, timpani\n**Tempo Range**: 60-100 BPM (heavy, deliberate, weighty)\n**Key/Mode**: E-flat minor (deep, sonorous, the key of brass gravitas)\n**Mood**: Titanic, ominous, godslayer, world-ending-consequence, cool-name-terrifying-power\n\n**How It Integrates**: Josh Coolbreeze's heavy woodwind theme is the most purely orchestral of all character themes -- it does not clash with the throughline, it OVERWHELMS it. When Josh acts (killing Asmodeus in Ch. 136), the standard orchestral forces are not enough. The entire woodwind section doubles down: bassoons in their lowest register, contrabassoons vibrating the subwoofer, French horns in unison. The Raven Queen flute motif is consumed by the larger woodwind mass -- fate swallowed by consequence.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. His theme is the sound of a god dying -- the air leaving the room, the pressure dropping.\n\n**Reference Style**: Holst's \"Mars\" from The Planets for the militant heavy brass/woodwind quality. Hans Zimmer's Inception (BRAAAM) for the sub-bass impact. John Williams' Imperial March for the sense that something enormous is approaching. The name \"Coolbreeze\" is ironic -- his theme should feel like a hurricane.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: The killing of Asmodeus gets the score's single loudest moment -- every woodwind and brass instrument in unison on one chord, held for four beats, then absolute silence. The three specters escaping should get three descending woodwind runs (high to low) -- the sound of divine power scattering.\n\n---\n\n### Reynaldine -- Piano Live Ballad\n\n**Genre**: Piano ballad / romantic classical / art song\n**Primary Instruments**: Grand piano (solo), strings (entering gradually), voice (implied -- the melody is singable)\n**Tempo Range**: 50-80 BPM (rubato, emotionally responsive, never metronomic)\n**Key/Mode**: D-flat major (the key of Chopin nocturnes -- intimate, reflective, rich)\n**Mood**: Manipulative, beautiful, tragic, four-beings-in-one, the-most-dangerous-music-in-the-score\n\n**How It Integrates**: Reynaldine's piano is seductive. It enters the orchestral throughline as a solo voice -- delicate, beautiful, trustworthy. Then strings join. Then you realize the piano has been subtly altering the key of every other theme playing beneath it. Reynaldine's theme is the one that changes OTHER themes. The Raven Queen flute avoids his piano -- when Reynaldine plays, the flute goes silent. Fate does not enter this room.\n\n**Diegetic/Non-diegetic**: Non-diegetic. The piano is Reynaldine's true voice -- the four beings (Juliannia, Graz'zt, Gabriel, Reynardine) each contribute a voice to the texture. The left hand is Graz'zt (low, rumbling). The right hand melody alternates between Gabriel (ascending, angelic) and Juliannia (descending, fey). Reynardine is the sustain pedal -- connecting everything, blurring the boundaries.\n\n**Reference Style**: Chopin's Ballade No. 1 in G minor for the narrative arc within a single piece. Ryuichi Sakamoto (Merry Christmas Mr. Lawrence) for the devastating simplicity. For the four-beings-revealed moment, the piano splits into four independent voices like a Bach fugue -- beautiful and terrifying.\n\n**Character-Specific Details**: Nyx's theme distorts into a music-box version when Reynaldine is present. The \"Some things are worth fighting for\" line should be a piano phrase that directly quotes and inverts Randal's pacifist melody. The plane-collapse attempt gets the piano playing impossibly fast (like Liszt's transcendental etudes) -- technical virtuosity as cosmic ambition.\n\n---\n\n## Location Themes\n\n### Tavern (Argos, WindHaven, general)\n\n**Palette**: Acoustic guitar, fiddle, bodhr&aacute;n, tin whistle, lute\n**Tempo**: 100-130 BPM (lively)\n**Mode**: G Mixolydian (bright, festive, warm)\n**Feel**: Warm amber light rendered as sound. Think Witcher 3 tavern music -- the campfire glow of civilization. Diegetic musicians are present. Randal and Frozen Song may be performing. The Raven Queen flute appears only as a distant echo from outside the tavern walls.\n\n### Dungeon / Underdark\n\n**Palette**: Low strings (col legno, sul ponticello), prepared piano, waterphone, subharmonic bass\n**Tempo**: 40-70 BPM (crawling, oppressive)\n**Mode**: Locrian (the most dissonant mode -- nothing feels resolved)\n**Feel**: Silence is the primary instrument. Sound design dominates: dripping water, distant echoes, the groan of stone. When music enters, it is sparse and wrong -- intervals too wide, rhythm too irregular. Think Akira Yamaoka (Silent Hill) for the industrial-horror texture. The bioluminescent blue-green of the Deep should be audible as high-frequency tonal shimmers.\n\n### City (Argos, Cloud City, Magister's Circle)\n\n**Palette**: Full orchestra (lighter), percussion (market bustle), bells, street musician elements\n**Tempo**: 90-120 BPM (bustling)\n**Mode**: D major (bright, civic, purposeful)\n**Feel**: Layered -- multiple diegetic sources (market vendors, street performers, church bells) competing beneath a non-diegetic orchestral line. Each city has a sub-variant: Argos is saltier (accordion, concertina), Cloud City is airier (glass harmonica, high strings, wind sounds), the Magister's Circle is more academic (harpsichord, chamber music).\n\n### Feywild\n\n**Palette**: Celesta, harp glissandi, reversed/time-stretched instruments, glass harmonica, detuned music box\n**Tempo**: Variable (time moves differently -- the tempo should breathe, accelerate, and decelerate unpredictably)\n**Mode**: Whole-tone scale (nothing resolves, everything floats)\n**Feel**: Beautiful and wrong. Every instrument should sound slightly off -- a piano with one note out of tune, a harp with one string too tight. Colors are oversaturated, and the music should feel the same. Think Joe Hisaishi (Spirited Away) meets Debussy's \"Prelude to the Afternoon of a Faun.\" Bergs' psychedelic rock elements bleed through here post-ascension.\n\n### Forest (Evallan, general wilderness)\n\n**Palette**: Uilleann pipes, nature sounds (integrated rhythmically), woodwinds, solo cello, gentle percussion\n**Tempo**: 70-100 BPM (breathing, organic)\n**Mode**: D Dorian (Brigit's key -- the forest is her domain)\n**Feel**: Brigit's Celtic theme is the foundation. Layers of nature sound design (birdsong, wind, creek water) become rhythmic elements. Think the quieter moments of the Witcher 3 Skellige music. The Zodiac Locket motif appears when the forest is ancient or magical.\n\n### Ocean / Ship (Thornberry Shade)\n\n**Palette**: Accordion/concertina, boomy low drums, shanty-style vocals (implied), fiddle, crashing wave percussion\n**Tempo**: 80-110 BPM (rolling, wave-paced -- 6/8 time signature)\n**Mode**: A Dorian (sea shanty key, minor but moving forward)\n**Feel**: The Thornberry Shade is home to both Group A and Group B. The sea theme should carry Nyx's fey shimmer (she glamours the ship), Varis's watchful guitar (he keeps watch), and the general party warmth. Think Hans Zimmer's Pirates of the Caribbean stripped of bombast -- the human moments between storms.\n\n### Vag'Mahar / Dwarven Stronghold\n\n**Palette**: Anvil percussion, low brass, war drums, chanting (implied), hammering rhythms\n**Tempo**: 80-100 BPM (marching, relentless)\n**Mode**: D minor (Daina's key -- the forge's domain)\n**Feel**: Daina and Bergs' themes converge here. The forge is audible -- rhythmic hammering as percussion, the roar of bellows as sustained bass. Think the Mines of Moria theme from Lord of the Rings but with more heat and industry. The Fireforge legacy made audible.\n\n---\n\n## Combat Music\n\n### Standard Encounter\n\n**Palette**: Full orchestra + character themes of combatants woven in\n**Tempo**: 120-140 BPM\n**Mode**: Varies by enemy type (undead = Phrygian, beasts = Aeolian, humanoid = minor keys)\n**Structure**: 8-bar intro (tension build) -> main theme (combat proper) -> dynamic sections that shift based on who is fighting. Daina's guitar enters when she rages. Sprocket's electronics surge when he casts. The combat theme should be modular -- pieces that can be mixed based on which PCs are engaged.\n\n### Boss Fight\n\n**Palette**: Full orchestra at maximum force + choir (implied, wordless) + the antagonist's specific instrumentation\n**Tempo**: 100-130 BPM (heavier, more deliberate than standard combat)\n**Mode**: Tritone-heavy (the \"devil's interval\" -- dissonance as threat)\n**Structure**: Three phases. Phase 1: the boss theme dominates, party themes suppressed. Phase 2: party themes begin to push through. Phase 3: party themes overtake the boss theme in a musical victory.\n\n**Boss-specific instrumentation**:\n- **Mordred/Ramses**: Pipe organ + distorted choir -- lich magic as corrupted religion\n- **Oblivion Wayne**: Nocturnus's emo theme inverted and slowed -- his family turned against him\n- **Peraxis Phyrexia**: Illithid psychic static -- granular synthesis, brain-scrambling frequencies\n- **Asmodeus**: Josh Coolbreeze's woodwind theme, but Josh's version is louder -- the god dies to the bigger sound\n- **Reynaldine (four beings)**: Four piano voices playing different pieces simultaneously\n\n### Chase Scene\n\n**Palette**: Driving percussion, ostinato strings, character themes at double tempo\n**Tempo**: 140-170 BPM (breathless)\n**Mode**: Mixolydian (forward momentum without full resolution)\n**Feel**: Think the Lupin III chase themes or Mission Impossible -- genre-agnostic excitement. Sprocket's chiptune and Tesca's tabla provide the rhythmic backbone. Comedy chase scenes get faster tempos and major keys. Life-or-death chases get minor keys and dissonance.\n\n---\n\n## Emotional Cues\n\n### Victory\n\n**Palette**: Full orchestra, brass fanfare, Raven Queen flute (triumphant variant), all active character themes in harmony\n**Tempo**: 100-120 BPM (measured, earned)\n**Key**: D major (the \"victory key\" -- bright, warm, satisfying)\n**Note**: Victory in Mardov always costs something. The fanfare should contain a minor-key shadow -- a single oboe line that mourns beneath the triumph.\n\n### Loss / Death\n\n**Palette**: Solo instrument (whichever character is affected), Raven Queen flute (dirge variant), sustained strings\n**Tempo**: 50-60 BPM (funereal)\n**Key**: F minor (grief)\n**Note**: The Raven Queen motif is most prominent during death scenes. It should feel like a hand reaching down -- not threatening, but inevitable. Sabine Ra chose this. She chose them.\n\n### Revelation / Prophecy\n\n**Palette**: Raven Queen flute (foreground, unaccompanied), then gradual orchestral swell, celesta, glass harmonica\n**Tempo**: Free time / rubato (time stops during revelation)\n**Key**: Whole-tone transition -> resolving to the relevant character's key\n**Note**: The three prophecies (Ruth, the Hags, Mia) each get their own variant. Ruth's is warm (gold arcane geometry). The Hags' is corrupt (organic purple wisps). Mia's is cosmic (indigo star-light).\n\n### Romance / Intimacy\n\n**Palette**: Solo acoustic instruments from the relevant characters' themes, minimal orchestration\n**Tempo**: 60-80 BPM (heartbeat)\n**Key**: Whatever key brings the two characters' themes into closest harmony\n**Note**: Romance in Mardov is rare and earned. Sprocket and Trixie get a chiptune waltz. Nyx and her lost sister get the inverted mirror theme. Brigit and the forest get a Celtic lullaby.\n\n### Betrayal\n\n**Palette**: The betrayer's theme, distorted and played against the victim's theme in dissonance\n**Tempo**: Decelerating (time slowing as trust breaks)\n**Key**: The victim's key, with the betrayer's key modulating against it\n**Note**: Dallor's betrayals get Stalwart's country fiddle played out of tune. Reynaldine's manipulations get his piano altering other themes' keys without permission. The party split (Ch. 41) gets every character theme playing simultaneously and then separating into silence.\n\n---\n\n## Production Notes\n\n### Instrument Priority for MusicGen/AI Generation\n\nWhen generating tracks, prioritize recognizable genre markers:\n1. The character's primary instrument (it must be identifiable within 5 seconds)\n2. The Raven Queen flute motif (woven in where specified)\n3. The dark fantasy orchestral throughline (strings + brass foundation)\n4. Genre-specific percussion and rhythm\n\n### Mixing Philosophy\n\n- Character themes should be mixed to emerge from and recede into the orchestral throughline\n- Never more than 2-3 character themes active simultaneously\n- The Raven Queen flute sits in the mid-high frequency range and should always cut through\n- Diegetic music (Randal, tavern) gets a different reverb space (room) vs non-diegetic (hall/plate)\n\n### Campaign Arc Across Score\n\n| Act | Musical Character | Orchestral Weight |\n|-----|-------------------|-------------------|\n| Act 1 | Simple, folk-leaning, themes introduced one at a time | Light chamber orchestra |\n| Act 2 | Themes begin interacting, sea/voyage textures | Medium orchestra, nautical elements |\n| Act 3 | Split: two parallel scores diverge | Full orchestra split into two palettes (blue-green vs amber) |\n| Act 4 | Themes converge, harmonic tension builds | Full orchestra unified, increasing dissonance |\n| Act 5 | All themes active, cosmic scale | Maximum orchestral force + all genre elements simultaneously |\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":877,"cache_read_input_tokens":67798,"output_tokens":9326,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":877},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[],"speed":"standard"}},"requestId":"req_011CZ9ge5WmJvT2HBRNztyTn","type":"assistant","uuid":"7b3430f3-639a-45ee-9b21-851c0bf0a288","timestamp":"2026-03-18T01:36:59.613Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"a5125120-9cbe-408a-953a-457fed0eba08","version":"2.1.78","gitBranch":"main","slug":"fluffy-cuddling-tome"}

{"parentUuid":"6d282478-dc2c-4559-8632-3f573c39fa0c","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a8bfbbbb3719cc3f4","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01PDYH8zdXakt7oTmisHuFQH","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01THZnfgvTzNtA9nYVYexQML","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager/mardov-vault/production/musicgen-prompts.md","content":"---\ntype: production\ntitle: \"Mardov Campaign — MusicGen API Prompts\"\ncreated: 2026-03-17\ntags:\n  - music\n  - production\n  - musicgen\n  - ai-audio\n---\n\n# MusicGen API Prompts\n\nReady-to-use text prompts for MusicGen / MusicLM / Stable Audio. Each character gets three variants (intro theme, action variant, emotional variant). Location and combat prompts follow.\n\nSee `music-direction.md` for the full creative rationale behind each assignment.\n\n---\n\n## Character Themes\n\n### Daina Fireforge (Heavy Metal)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\ndark fantasy heavy metal theme, down-tuned electric guitar, doom metal riff in D minor, double bass drums, anvil percussion hits, orchestral strings underneath, epic cinematic, 110 BPM, dark and powerful, forge fire atmosphere, dwarf warrior energy\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\naggressive thrash metal battle theme, fast double kick drums, heavy distorted power chords, 135 BPM, D minor, orchestral brass stabs between guitar riffs, anvil strikes on downbeats, volcanic intensity, rage and fury, cinematic fantasy combat, no vocals\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nslow doom metal ballad, clean electric guitar arpeggios over sustained orchestral strings, solo flute melody weaving above, 80 BPM, D minor, melancholic and grieving, distant anvil echoes, a warrior remembering her father, cinematic dark fantasy, atmospheric\n```\n\n---\n\n### Sprocket Overspark (Electronic)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nplayful electronic chiptune meets dark orchestral, arpeggiated synthesizer, 8-bit textures, glitchy digital sounds, ticking clock percussion, C major shifting to C minor, 140 BPM, manic genius energy, crystalline bell accents, cinematic fantasy with retro game music fusion\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nintense electronic battle theme, fast arpeggiated synths, glitch hop percussion, laser sound effects, 155 BPM, driving bass synth, orchestral strings underneath digital textures, cosmic and frenetic, wizard casting spells, chiptune meets epic orchestra, C minor\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nmelancholic chiptune ambient, slow arpeggiated synthesizer, solo flute melody over gentle electronic pads, music box texture, 90 BPM, C minor, bittersweet and cosmic, a small creature carrying the weight of fate, gentle orchestral strings, nostalgic 8-bit sadness\n```\n\n---\n\n### Brigit Wildfire (Celtic)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nceltic folk orchestral theme, uilleann pipes melody, bodhran drum rhythm, tin whistle harmony, fiddle countermelody, D Dorian mode, 100 BPM, warm and fierce, campfire atmosphere, dark fantasy with Irish folk roots, nature sounds undertone, cinematic\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nfast celtic battle reel, driving fiddle and tin whistle, bodhran at double tempo, 130 BPM, D Dorian, orchestral strings joining celtic instruments, fire crackling percussion, druidic energy, wildfire transformation, pipes becoming a war drone, epic fantasy combat\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nslow celtic air, solo uilleann pipes lament, gentle harp arpeggios, distant bodhran heartbeat, 70 BPM, D Dorian, deeply emotional and rooted, a woman discovering she is something ancient, orchestral strings swelling beneath folk instruments, bittersweet forest twilight\n```\n\n---\n\n### Varis Laindon (Soft Rock)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\ngentle soft rock with orchestral backdrop, fingerpicked acoustic guitar, clean electric guitar with reverb, brushed drums, 85 BPM, G major, warm and melancholic, a ranger watching the sunrise, steady and protective, folk rock meets dark fantasy orchestra, dawn light atmosphere\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nbuilding soft rock power ballad into full orchestra, sustained power chord with orchestral brass, driving bass guitar, 110 BPM, E minor, an arrow being drawn in slow motion, tension building to explosive release, folk rock crescendo, cinematic fantasy action, shimmering harmonics\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nmelancholic fingerpicked acoustic guitar solo, solo flute above gentle strings, 65 BPM, E minor, a soldier keeping watch alone at dawn, deeply personal and quiet, reverb-heavy atmosphere, loss and hope coexisting, Nick Drake style folk meets orchestral dark fantasy\n```\n\n---\n\n### Nocturnus Wayne (Emo)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\ngothic emo rock with orchestral undertones, distorted guitar with heavy gain, driving bass eighth notes, crash cymbal hits, 120 BPM, B-flat minor, brooding and explosive, dark knight vampire energy, My Chemical Romance meets dark fantasy orchestra, dramatic and self-destructive\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\naggressive post-hardcore battle theme, fast distorted guitars, blast beat drums, 140 BPM, B-flat minor, gothic rock meets epic orchestra, screaming guitar leads, vampiric fury, blade combat intensity, orchestral choir underneath punk aggression, dark and relentless\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nethereal ambient emo ballad, clean guitar with heavy reverb, sparse piano notes, 75 BPM, B-flat minor, translucent and fragile, a vampire remembering being human, Radiohead style atmospheric, solo flute melody descending, ghostly strings, deeply melancholic dark fantasy\n```\n\n---\n\n### Nyx Khamestria (Classical Ballet)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nromantic classical ballet theme, solo violin melody, celesta accents, harp arpeggios, pizzicato strings, 90 BPM in 3/4 waltz time, F-sharp minor, graceful and melancholic, fey magic shimmer, Tchaikovsky meets dark fantasy, delicate and otherworldly, a hidden soul dancing alone\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\ndramatic ballet orchestral, full strings fortissimo, solo violin aggressive bowing, celesta trills, 120 BPM in 6/8, F-sharp minor, fey energy crackling, warlock power unleashed through classical form, Stravinsky-like rhythmic intensity, dark fantasy ballet combat, wild and precise\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nheartbreaking solo violin over sustained strings, celesta music box texture, 60 BPM rubato, F-sharp minor, a twin separated from her sister, Swan Lake melancholy, single cello joining the violin in duet, dark fantasy lullaby, Ghibli-style emotional orchestral, fading to silence\n```\n\n---\n\n### Stalwart Little Goldentooth (Country)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nepic country rock with orchestral strings, pedal steel guitar, banjo rhythm, strummed acoustic guitar, 100 BPM, A major, righteous and warm, golden paladin energy, southern rock meets fantasy orchestra, earnest and powerful, Johnny Cash meets Lord of the Rings\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\ndriving southern rock battle theme, heavy pedal steel, power chords on electric guitar, crashing drums, 130 BPM, A major, divine radiant energy expressed as country rock, shield bash percussion hits, gospel choir undertone, righteous fury, fantasy combat with Americana soul\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\ngentle country ballad, fingerpicked acoustic guitar, soft pedal steel swells, harmonica melody, 75 BPM, A minor, a protector who cannot protect everyone, orphan's lament, warm and sad, Chris Stapleton emotional depth, gentle orchestral strings joining, quiet faith\n```\n\n---\n\n### Bergs Fireforge (Psychedelic Rock)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\npsychedelic rock with orchestral elements, fuzz guitar, Hammond organ, wah pedal, bongo drums, 90 BPM, E Mixolydian, earthy and cosmic, mushroom spores floating as sound, Pink Floyd meets dark fantasy, a dwarf becoming something more, warm and expansive, phaser effects\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nheavy psychedelic battle rock, thick fuzz guitar riffs, driving organ, sitar drone underneath, 115 BPM, E Mixolydian, cosmic forge energy, blacksmith precision as rhythm, fungal network pulsing, space rock meets fantasy combat, Black Sabbath Planet Caravan intensity, transcendent\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nambient psychedelic meditation, gentle sitar and tanpura drone, organ pads, soft bongo rhythm, 70 BPM, E Mixolydian, a god who is still a dwarf, bioluminescent spore atmosphere, Tame Impala dreamy production, connection across consciousness, deeply warm and vast, ascending slowly\n```\n\n---\n\n### Randal (Singer-Songwriter Guitar -- Diegetic)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nintimate acoustic singer-songwriter, fingerpicked guitar, panflute melody, gentle hand drum, 80 BPM, C major, warm tavern atmosphere, a traveling bard playing by lantern light, Nick Drake folk style, unhurried and wise, no orchestral backing just pure acoustic, intimate recording\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nurgent acoustic folk with building intensity, strummed acoustic guitar driving forward, panflute in countermelody, tambourine percussion, 100 BPM, A minor, a bard's counter-charm breaking a spell, folk music as magic, the moment acoustic becomes epic, strings gradually joining underneath\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nsolo fingerpicked acoustic guitar, extremely gentle, 60 BPM slowing gradually, C major fading to A minor, a dying man playing his last song, each note deliberate and precious, distant flute echo, no drums no bass, just one guitar in a quiet room, lantern light as sound, mortality and peace\n```\n\n---\n\n### Tesca Duskhallow (Yoga/Meditation)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nmeditation ambient world music, singing bowls, tabla rhythm, tanpura drone, bamboo flute melody, gentle bells, 70 BPM, D Phrygian mode, centered and flowing, twilight colors as sound, a monk at dawn, Eastern meditation meets dark fantasy, peaceful surface with warrior underneath\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nintense martial arts percussion with meditation undertones, fast tabla patterns, bamboo flute aggressive melody, finger cymbals accenting strikes, 130 BPM, D Phrygian, kung fu movie energy, meditation music turned to combat, Crouching Tiger soundtrack style, flowing and deadly, no pause between strikes\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\ngentle singing bowl meditation, single tanpura drone, very soft bamboo flute, 50 BPM, D Phrygian, a street kid finding stillness for the first time, monastery at sunset, deeply peaceful and sad, parents lost to fire, meditation as survival, minimal and spacious, breathing rhythm\n```\n\n---\n\n### Birt (Detective / Noir Jazz)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nnoir jazz detective theme, cool alto saxophone melody, muted trumpet harmony, walking upright bass, brushed snare drums, cocktail piano, 95 BPM, E-flat major, wry and sophisticated, a bird wizard in a trench coat energy, Cowboy Bebop jazz style, smoky and clever, dark fantasy noir\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\naggressive bebop jazz battle theme, fast saxophone runs, driving upright bass, crashing ride cymbal, piano comping hard, 130 BPM, C minor, high-level wizard devastation with jazz cool, rapid-fire spell casting as bebop improvisation, Yoko Kanno intensity, feathers and fire\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nslow jazz ballad, solo muted trumpet over gentle piano chords, 65 BPM, E-flat minor, a bird who misses flying just to fly, brushed drums barely audible, Vince Guaraldi tenderness, the comedy stripped away revealing something real underneath, dark fantasy lament in jazz form\n```\n\n---\n\n### Josh Coolbreeze (Heavy Woodwind Orchestral)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nheavy orchestral woodwind theme, deep bassoon and contrabassoon melody, bass clarinet harmony, French horns in unison, timpani rolls, 75 BPM, E-flat minor, titanic and ominous, a godslayer approaches, Holst Mars energy, sub-bass rumble, the air pressure dropping, massive and deliberate\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nthunderous orchestral climax, full woodwind section fortissimo, French horns blaring, contrabassoon shaking the floor, timpani and bass drum, 90 BPM, E-flat minor, killing a god, Hans Zimmer BRAAAM energy, maximum orchestral force, every brass and woodwind instrument at full power, world-ending consequence\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nsolo bassoon lament over sustained strings, 55 BPM, E-flat minor, the weight of having killed a god, French horn joining in gentle harmony, quiet after the storm, deeply solemn and heavy, the consequences settling, a cool breeze after a hurricane, orchestral dark fantasy funeral march\n```\n\n---\n\n### Reynaldine (Piano Ballad)\n\n**Intro Theme**\n```\nsolo grand piano ballad, romantic classical, Chopin nocturne style, rubato tempo around 65 BPM, D-flat major, beautiful and manipulative, four voices woven into one piano, seductive and dangerous, a fey lord's true voice, intimate and unsettling, each phrase slightly different from expected\n```\n\n**Action Variant**\n```\nvirtuosic piano with full orchestra, Liszt transcendental etude speed, 140 BPM, D-flat major to chromatic chaos, four independent piano voices simultaneously, Bach fugue structure at Romantic era intensity, cosmic ambition as technical brilliance, planes collapsing into music, terrifying beauty\n```\n\n**Emotional Variant**\n```\nsparse piano with single sustained string note, 50 BPM rubato, D-flat major, a being who is four people and none of them, each chord slightly detuned, Ryuichi Sakamoto simplicity, heartbreaking and cold, the piano that changes every theme it touches, alone in a room, a manipulator with no one left to manipulate\n```\n\n---\n\n## The Raven Queen Motif (Standalone)\n\n**Pure Statement**\n```\nsolo flute melody, five descending notes in minor key, haunting and simple, 70 BPM free time, no accompaniment, the sound of fate reaching down, ancient and inevitable, a goddess who chose to shatter, dark fantasy, the thread that connects everything, piccolo trill at the end\n```\n\n**Orchestral Swell Variant**\n```\nsolo flute melody joined by full woodwind choir, five-note descending motif harmonized across flutes oboes clarinets bassoons, building orchestral swell underneath, 80 BPM, dark fantasy prophecy moment, cosmic revelation, the fate of the world in a melody, celesta accents, breathtaking and terrifying\n```\n\n**Corrupted/Vecna Variant**\n```\ndistorted flute melody through heavy effects processing, the five-note motif played backwards and pitch-shifted down, dark ambient drone underneath, glitch textures, 60 BPM, the Raven Queen motif as heard through Vecna's realm, corrupted fate, wrong and familiar, horror fantasy atmosphere\n```\n\n---\n\n## Location Themes\n\n### Tavern\n\n```\nmedieval tavern folk music, acoustic guitar strumming, fiddle melody, bodhran rhythm, tin whistle harmony, lute arpeggios, 115 BPM, G Mixolydian, warm and lively, ale and firelight, fantasy pub atmosphere, Witcher 3 tavern energy, celebratory and rowdy, acoustic only\n```\n\n### Dungeon / Underdark\n\n```\ndark ambient dungeon atmosphere, low droning strings, prepared piano metallic textures, dripping water sounds, subharmonic bass rumble, 50 BPM, Locrian mode, oppressive and claustrophobic, bioluminescent blue-green sonic shimmer, Silent Hill horror meets fantasy dungeon, sparse and terrifying\n```\n\n### City (General)\n\n```\nbustling medieval city orchestral theme, full orchestra light and energetic, bells ringing, percussion suggesting market crowds, 105 BPM, D major, civic and purposeful, fantasy city daytime, multiple layers of activity, bright brass fanfare, strings providing forward motion, hopeful and busy\n```\n\n### Feywild\n\n```\nethereal detuned orchestral, celesta and glass harmonica, harp glissandi, reversed and time-stretched instruments, whole-tone scale, variable tempo breathing between 60-90 BPM, beautiful and wrong, oversaturated fairy realm, Debussy meets Spirited Away, psychedelic shimmer, nothing resolves, dreamlike and unsettling\n```\n\n### Forest\n\n```\ngentle forest ambience with celtic orchestral, uilleann pipes soft melody, solo cello, nature sounds woven into rhythm, woodwind bird calls, 80 BPM, D Dorian, ancient forest at twilight, druidic atmosphere, organic and breathing, leaves rustling as percussion, the Evallan Forest, peaceful but watching\n```\n\n### Ocean / Thornberry Shade\n\n```\nnautical fantasy theme, accordion melody, deep boomy drums suggesting waves, fiddle sea shanty style, 95 BPM in 6/8 time signature, A Dorian, rolling oceanic feel, wooden ship creaking, salt air atmosphere, Pirates of the Caribbean human moments, adventurous but melancholic, seafaring fellowship\n```\n\n### Dwarven Stronghold / Vag'Mahar\n\n```\ndwarven forge orchestral, heavy anvil percussion, low brass fanfare, war drums marching rhythm, chanting undertone, hammering as beat, 90 BPM, D minor, industrial and ancient, fire and metal, deep underground citadel, Lord of the Rings Moria energy but with more heat, forging as worship\n```\n\n### Shadowfell\n\n```\ndark atmospheric void, reversed strings, whispering textures, hollow wind, extremely sparse piano notes with long decay, 40 BPM, F minor, the place between death and un-death, cold and empty, echo of the living world heard through stone, Raven Queen's domain, deeply unsettling ambient horror\n```\n\n---\n\n## Combat Themes\n\n### Standard Encounter\n\n```\nepic fantasy battle orchestra, driving strings ostinato, brass fanfare, war drums, full percussion section, 130 BPM, D minor, cinematic combat energy, swords clashing as percussion, dynamic and modular, medieval fantasy action, heroic but dangerous, Bear McCreary battle style\n```\n\n### Boss Fight (Phase 1 -- Villain Dominant)\n\n```\ndark epic orchestral boss theme, menacing low brass, dissonant tritone intervals, thunderous timpani, oppressive choir undertone, 110 BPM, chromatic and unstable, the enemy is winning, overwhelming dark power, Hans Zimmer villain energy, massive sub-bass impacts, relentless and crushing\n```\n\n### Boss Fight (Phase 2 -- Heroes Rising)\n\n```\nbuilding epic orchestral, heroic brass melody emerging through darkness, strings gaining strength, drums shifting from oppressive to driving, 120 BPM, transitioning from minor to major, the heroes pushing back, hope breaking through, all character instruments faintly audible, cinematic fantasy climax building\n```\n\n### Boss Fight (Phase 3 -- Victory)\n\n```\ntriumphant full orchestra, all brass and strings at maximum, heroic main theme fortissimo, 125 BPM, D major, the heroes have won but at cost, celebration with mourning undertone, solo flute above the orchestra, a single oboe lament beneath the fanfare, bittersweet fantasy victory, earned not given\n```\n\n### Chase Scene\n\n```\nfast-paced fantasy chase theme, driving string ostinato at double speed, urgent brass stabs, rapid percussion, 155 BPM, Mixolydian mode, breathless pursuit, comedic energy possible, Lupin III chase style meets fantasy orchestra, running through medieval streets, exciting and relentless\n```\n\n---\n\n## Emotional Cues\n\n### Victory Fanfare\n\n```\norchestral victory fanfare, bright brass melody, full strings, triumphant percussion, solo flute descending motif above, 110 BPM, D major, hard-won triumph, medieval fantasy celebration, a cost beneath the joy, warm and bittersweet, cinematic resolution\n```\n\n### Death / Loss\n\n```\nfuneral orchestral dirge, solo flute playing the Raven Queen five-note motif, sustained strings in F minor, 50 BPM, deeply sorrowful, the hand of fate reaching down, dark fantasy grief, a single violin joining the flute, inevitable and gentle, not cruel but final\n```\n\n### Prophecy / Revelation\n\n```\ncosmic orchestral revelation, solo flute unaccompanied beginning, celesta and glass harmonica entering, whole-tone scale dissolving into tonal resolution, rubato free tempo, stars aligning as sound, ancient knowledge revealed, dark fantasy divination, breathtaking orchestral swell, time stopping\n```\n\n### Romance / Intimacy\n\n```\ngentle intimate acoustic duet, two solo instruments trading phrases, minimal orchestration, 65 BPM, major key, heartbeat bass drum, tender and vulnerable, two people choosing each other in a dying world, dark fantasy love theme, quiet and real, no grandeur just truth\n```\n\n### Betrayal\n\n```\ndissonant orchestral shock, two themes colliding in wrong keys, decelerating tempo from 100 to 60 BPM, trust shattering as sound, string cluster chords, the moment realization hits, dark fantasy dramatic twist, sickeningly beautiful, a theme you trusted becoming something else\n```\n\n### The Party Split (Chapter 41)\n\n```\nbittersweet orchestral separation, multiple instrument themes playing simultaneously then pulling apart stereo left and right, decelerating from 90 to 60 BPM, every character audible then fading one by one, deep sadness, the party dividing, dark fantasy farewell, two color palettes separating, ending in silence\n```\n\n### The Reunion\n\n```\nbuilding orchestral reunion theme, two separate musical palettes approaching from opposite sides, cool blue-green ambient meeting warm amber folk, accelerating from 70 to 100 BPM, recognition and relief, instruments finding each other again, celtic folk meeting psychedelic rock meeting jazz meeting metal, all themes harmonizing for the first time in acts, tears of joy as orchestral crescendo\n```\n\n---\n\n## Act-Specific Atmosphere Prompts\n\n### Act 1 -- The Awakening (Light Orchestra)\n\n```\nsimple dark fantasy chamber orchestra, small string ensemble, solo woodwinds, gentle introduction of themes one at a time, 90 BPM, intimate and discovering, a party forming, the world is still small, cinematic fantasy beginning, hopeful beneath the darkness, folk elements emerging naturally\n```\n\n### Act 2 -- The Voyage (Medium Orchestra + Sea)\n\n```\nnautical dark fantasy orchestra, medium ensemble with sea shanty undertones, accordion and fiddle woven into orchestral strings, 100 BPM, themes beginning to interact and harmonize, oceanic journey, the world expanding, zodiac spirits as cosmic shimmer, adventure with weight, growing into something larger\n```\n\n### Act 3 -- The Escalation (Split Score)\n\n```\ndivided orchestral palette, two simultaneous scores, cool bioluminescent underground ambient left channel, warm amber surface folk-orchestra right channel, themes separated and developing independently, 95 BPM, parallel narratives, character arcs deepening in isolation, the longest absence, dark fantasy at full complexity\n```\n\n### Act 4 -- The Endgame (Full Convergence)\n\n```\nfull dark fantasy orchestra converging, all themes present simultaneously, increasing harmonic tension, 105 BPM, building toward something irreversible, gods dying, heroes rising,
